\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}}(\mathcal{W}_{\phi}f)(a,b)\left((\mathcal{W}_{\phi}g)(a,b)\right)^{c}\frac{dadb}{a^{2}}=c_{\phi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(x)(g(x))^{c}dx,

(347)

followed by the inversion formula
f(x)=\frac{1}{c_{\phi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left(\left(f\ostar\check{\hat{\phi}}_{a}\right)\ostar\phi_{a}\right)(x)\frac{da}{a^{2}}
in
L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}).
If the WT of
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
is defined as
(\check{\tilde{\phi}}_{a}\ostar f)(b),
then the definition results in the property
\mathcal{W}_{\phi}(f\ostar g)=\left(\mathcal{W}_{\phi}f\right)\ostar g
for
f,g\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
which was not the case when we take the definition as (
346
). But for definition
(\check{\tilde{\phi}}_{a}\ostar f)(b),
the Parseval’s formula and the inversion formula cannot be achieved as in the case of the transform (
346
).

23
Quaternion Stockwell Transform (QST)

Akila et al.
[
4
]
introduced the QST for a function
f=f_{1}+jf_{2}\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
with respect to
\phi=\phi_{1}+j\phi_{2}\in L^{1}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})\cap L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
as

\displaystyle(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f)(a,b)=\left([f]_{a}\ostar\mathcal{M}_{a}\check{\tilde{\phi}}\right)(b),\penalty\ (a,b)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}

(348)

where
[f]_{a}(x)=f(x)e^{-iax},

(\mathcal{M}_{a}f)(x)=|a|f(ax),

\tilde{\phi}=\bar{\phi}_{1}-j\check{\phi}_{2},

f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
and
\ostar
denotes the quaternion convolution given by (
142
). The definition (
348
) can be equivalently written as

\displaystyle(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f)(a,b)=\left(\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{1}}f_{1}+\mathcal{S}_{\check{\bar{\phi}}_{2}}\check{\bar{f}}_{2}+j\left(\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{1}}f_{2}-\mathcal{S}_{\check{\bar{\phi}}_{2}}\check{\bar{f}}_{1}\right)\right)(a,b)

(349)

In view of equation (
349
), it follows that the definition (
348
) is consistent with the definition of the classical WT on
L^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{R}).
The transform is linear in
f
with the scalars in
\mathbb{H}
and has the translation property

(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}(\tau_{b_{0}}f))(a,b)=\left(\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{1}}(f_{1}+jf_{2})\right)(a,b-b_{0})-j\left(\mathcal{S}_{\check{\hat{\phi}}_{2}}(\check{\hat{f}}_{2}+j\check{\hat{f}}_{2})\right)(a,b+b_{0})

and the scaling property
(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}(f(\lambda x)))(a,b)=(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f)(\frac{a}{\lambda},\lambda b),
for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}_{0}.
Using the convolution theorem associated with the convolution
\ostar
(
142
) and the condition that
c_{\phi}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\star}}\frac{|\hat{\phi}(\xi-1)|^{2}}{|\xi|}d\xi
in non-zero finite, authors obtained the Parseval’s formula for the QST (
348
) for the function
f,g\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\star}}(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f)(a,b)\left((\mathcal{S}_{\phi}g)(a,b)\right)^{c}\frac{dadb}{|a|}=2\pi c_{\phi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(x)(g(x))^{c}dx,

(350)

using which they obtained the inversion formula
f(x)=\frac{1}{2\pi c_{\phi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\star}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f)(a,b)\Phi_{a,b}(x)\frac{dbda}{|a|}
in
L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}),
where
\hat{\Phi}_{a,b}(\eta)=e^{ib(a-\eta)\hat{\phi}\left(\frac{\eta-a}{a}\right)}
and characterized the range and proved that
\mathcal{S}_{\phi}\left(L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})\right)
is a RKHS with the kernel
K_{\phi}(a,b;a^{\prime},b^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Phi_{a,b}(x)\left(\Phi_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}(x)\right)^{c}dx

Kamel et al.
[
109
]
extended the definition (
237
) of the multidimensional ST to the quaternion valued function
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with respect to the window
\phi\in L^{1}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\cap L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).
They called the transform the two-dimensional QST, which is defined as

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f\right)(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})=|\det A_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-ix_{1}\xi_{1}}f(\boldsymbol{x})e^{-jx_{2}\xi_{2}}\overline{\phi\left(A_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{b})\right)}d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{x}),

(351)

where
d\mu_{d}(\boldsymbol{x})=\frac{d\boldsymbol{x}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{d}{2}}},

A_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}
is a
2\times 2
matrix
diag(\xi_{1},\xi_{2}),\penalty\ \xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in\mathbb{R}.
Authors in
[
109
]
obtained some basic properties like linearity in
f
with respect to the complex scalars, anti-linearity in
\phi
with respect to the quaternion scalars and also the scaling formula
\left(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f\right)(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})=\frac{1}{|\lambda|}\left(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f\right)(\frac{\boldsymbol{\xi}}{\lambda},\lambda\boldsymbol{b}),\penalty\ \lambda\in\mathbb{R}_{0}.
Furthermore, motivated by
[
167
]
, as in the case of ST (
237
), author obtained the inversion formula for the transform (
351
), with the assumption that the window function
\phi
satisfies
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(\boldsymbol{x})d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})=1,
as
f=\mathcal{F}_{s}^{-1}A\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f,
where
\mathcal{F}_{s}
is given by (
122
). With the assumption that the quaternion window
\psi
(and also
\phi
) satisfies the conditions

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}_{s}\psi)(\boldsymbol{\omega})e^{-jb_{1}\omega_{1}}=e^{-jb_{2}\omega_{2}}(\mathcal{F}_{s}\psi)(\boldsymbol{\omega})

(352)

or

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}_{s}(j\psi))(\boldsymbol{\omega})e^{-jb_{1}\omega_{1}}=je^{-jb_{2}\omega_{2}}(\mathcal{F}_{s}\psi)(\boldsymbol{\omega})

(353)

authors also proved the Parseval’s formula for the QST (
351
) as
\langle\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f,\mathcal{S}_{\psi}g\rangle_{2,\mathbb{R}^{4}}=\langle fC^{\prime}_{\phi,\psi},g\rangle_{2,\mathbb{R}^{2}},
where
\langle F,G\rangle_{2,\mathbb{R}^{d}}=Sc\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}F(\boldsymbol{t})(G(\boldsymbol{t}))^{c}d\mu_{d}(\boldsymbol{t})\right],

C^{\prime}_{\phi,\psi}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\mathcal{F}_{s}\bar{\phi}\right)(1-\boldsymbol{\xi})\overline{\left(\mathcal{F}_{s}\bar{\psi}\right)(1-\boldsymbol{\xi})}\frac{d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{\xi})}{|\det A_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}|}
is a non-zero quaternion constant. With the notation
C^{\prime}_{\phi}=C^{\prime}_{\phi,\phi}
and
C^{\prime}_{\psi}=C^{\prime}_{\psi,\psi}
associated respectively with
two quaternion window
\phi
and
\psi
satisfying (
352
) or (
353
), it follows that for every
f,g\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
the function
(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})\rightarrow(\mathcal{S}_{\psi}f)(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}g)(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})
belongs to
L^{p}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}),\penalty\ p\geq 1
and satisfies the Lieb’s inequality

\displaystyle\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f)(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})(\mathcal{S}_{\psi}g)(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})\right|^{p}d\mu_{4}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})\bigg)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\left(C^{\prime}_{\phi}C^{\prime}_{\psi}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|f(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|g(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\phi(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\right)^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\psi(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\right)^{1-\frac{1}{p}}.

Moreover, if
\phi
be an quaternion window satisfying the assumptions (
352
) or (
353
), then for every
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
the transform
\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f
given by (
351
) belong to
L^{p}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
for
p\geq 2
and satisfies

\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f)(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})\right|^{p}d\mu_{4}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})\bigg)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq(C^{\prime}_{\phi})^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|f(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\phi(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)}

as a consequence of which it follows that, if
\mathcal{S}_{\psi}f,
for
f\neq 0,
is
\epsilon-
concentrated on a measurable set
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
of non-zero finite measure
\mu_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}(\Omega),
where
\mu_{4}
is a measure defined on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
given by
d\mu_{4}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})=d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{\xi})d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{b}),
then for every
p>2,

\mu_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}(\Omega)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\phi(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\geq C^{\prime}_{\psi}(1-\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{p}{p-2}}.

This is the Lieb’s UP associated with the QST (
351
).

Uncertainty Principles:
Using the UP for the QFT
[
6
]
, authors in
[
4
]
obtained the Heisenberg’s UP for the QST (
348
)

\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\star}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|b(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f)(a,b)|^{2}dbda\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\xi^{2}|\hat{f}(\xi)|^{2}d\xi\right)\geq c_{\phi}\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})},

for
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}).
Author in
[
109
]
also obtained the associated Beckner’s UP in terms of entropy as

E\left(|\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f|^{2}\right)\geq C^{\prime}_{\phi}\|f\|^{2}_{2,\mathbb{R}^{2}}\ln\left(\frac{1}{\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|f(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\phi(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\right)}\right),

where
E\left(|\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f|^{2}\right)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\ln\left(|(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f)(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})|^{2}\right)|(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f)(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})|^{2}d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{\xi})d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{b})
followed by the generalized Heisenberg UP for the QST

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\boldsymbol{\omega}|^{p}\left|\left(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f\right)(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{b})\right|^{2}d\mu_{4}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})\right)^{\frac{q}{p+q}}

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\boldsymbol{b}|^{q}\left|\left(\mathcal{S}_{\phi}f\right)(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{b})\right|^{2}d\mu_{4}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{b})\right)^{\frac{p}{p+q}}

\displaystyle\geq E_{p,q}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|f(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|g(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\mu_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\right),

where
E_{p,q}=\frac{2}{ep}\left(\frac{p}{q}\right)^{\frac{p}{p+q}}\left(\frac{pq}{\Gamma(\frac{2}{p})\Gamma(\frac{2}{q})}\right)^{\frac{pq}{2(p+q)}}
and
p,q
are positive real numbers.

24
Quaternion Windowed Fractional Fourier Transform (QWFrFT)

Using the idea given in
[
6
]
for defining the QWFT (see (
337
)), Rajkumar
[
164
]
defined the QWFrFT for the function
f=f_{1}+jf_{2}\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
with respect to a non-zero window function
g=g_{1}+jg_{2}\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
is as

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{G}_{g,\alpha}f\right)(\omega,b)=\left(G_{g_{1},\alpha}f_{1}+G_{\bar{g_{2}},\alpha}\bar{f_{2}}+j\left(G_{g_{1},\alpha}f_{2}+G_{\bar{g_{2}},\alpha}\bar{f_{1}}\right)\right)(\omega,b).

(354)

From equation (
354
), it follows that the transform is consistent with the classical WFrFT (
254
) whenever
f_{2}=g_{2}=0
and is
\mathbb{H}-
linear on
L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}).
Using the translation and dilation formula for the WFrFT of complex-valued functions, author obtain the corresponding formula as

\left(\mathcal{G}_{g,\alpha}(\tau_{t_{0}}f)\right)(\omega,b)=\left(\mathcal{G}_{g,\alpha}f\right)(\omega-t_{0}\cos\alpha,b-t_{0})e^{i\frac{t_{0}^{2}}{2}\sin\alpha\cos\alpha-it_{0}\omega\sin\alpha}

and

\left(\mathcal{G}_{g,\alpha}(\mathcal{M}_{\omega_{0}}f)\right)(\omega,b)=\left(\mathcal{G}_{g,\alpha}f\right)(\omega-\omega_{0}\sin\alpha,b)e^{i\frac{\omega_{0}^{2}}{2}\sin\alpha\cosh\alpha+i\omega_{0}\omega\cos\alpha},

where
(\mathcal{M}_{\omega_{0}}f)(t)=e^{-i\omega_{0}t}f(t).
Based on the relation
\left(\mathcal{G}_{g,\alpha}f\right)(\omega,b)=(\mathcal{F}_{\alpha}\{f(\tau)g(\tau-t)\})(\xi),
author obtained the Parseval’s formula
\left(\mathcal{G}_{g,\alpha}f,\mathcal{G}_{g,\alpha}h\right)=\|g\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})}(f,h),
for
f,h\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}),
as a consequence of which it follows that
\|\mathcal{G}_{g,\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R})}=\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})},
here the inner product
(\cdot,\cdot)
is given by (
8
). Looking forward for the inversion formula for the transform (
354
) author obtain the
2-D
inversion formula for
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
and
g\neq 0
as

f(x)=\frac{1}{\|g\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\mathcal{G}_{g,\alpha}f\right)(\omega,b)K_{-\alpha}(x,\omega)[g(x-b)]^{c}d\omega db,\penalty\ a.e.

Motivated by the idea of the
1-D
inversion formula for the classical WFT
[
196
]
, they also obtained the
1-D
inversion formula for the QWFT for the function
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
with assumption that
g=g_{1}+jg_{2}\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
is continuous in a neighborhood of
0
and
g(0)\neq 0
as

f(b)=\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\mathcal{G}_{g,\alpha}f\right)(\omega,b)K_{-\alpha}(b,\omega)d\omega\right]\frac{1}{g_{1}(0)+j\bar{g_{2}}(0)},\penalty\ a.e.

It is to be noted that although the inversion formula for the classical case (
255
) in
[
196
]
is similar, but it lacks rigor, because as
g\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{R})
its value at the single point cannot be determined. Thus to overcome this issue Rajkumar
[
164
]
while giving the inversion in
1-D
case for the transform (
354
) considered the function
g\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
in such a way that
g
is continuous in a neighborhood of
0
and
g(0)\neq 0.

Uncertainty Principles:
Based on the UP for the QFrFT
\mathcal{F}_{s}
given by

\|\xi\left(\mathcal{F}_{s}f\right)(\xi)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})}\|\omega\left(\mathcal{F}_{s}f\right)(\omega)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})}\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}|\sin(\alpha-\beta)|\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

author also obtained the Heisenberg’s UP for the QWFT (
354
) as

\|\omega\left(\mathcal{G}_{g,\alpha}f\right)(\omega,b)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R})}\|tf(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})}\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}|\sin\alpha|\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})}.

25
Quaternion Fractional Wavelet Transform (QFrWT)

A function
\psi\in L^{1}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})\cap L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
is said to be a quaternion admissible wavelet if it satisfies the condition that
0<C_{\psi,\alpha}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{|(\mathcal{F}_{\alpha}\psi)(\xi)|^{2}}{|\xi|}d\xi<\infty.
Roopkumar
[
169
]
introduced a QWT for the function
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
with respect to the quaternion admissible wavelet
\psi
as

\displaystyle(\mathcal{W}_{\psi,\alpha}f)(a,b)=(f\otimes_{\alpha}[U_{\alpha}\psi_{a}])(b),\penalty\ a,b\in\mathbb{R}

(355)

where
\otimes
is the convolution given by (
147
),
\psi_{a}(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{|a|}}\psi\left(-\frac{t}{a}\right)
and
U_{\alpha}
is defined by
U_{\alpha}(h)=\mathcal{F}_{-2\alpha}\overline{h_{1}}-jh_{2},\penalty\ h=h_{1}+jh_{2}.

Using the machinery of the QFrFT (
145
), author proved that the
\mathbb{H}-
linear transform (
355
) satisfies the Parseval’s identity
(\mathcal{W}_{\psi,\alpha}f,\mathcal{W}_{\psi,\alpha}g)=2\pi C_{\psi,\alpha}(f,g)
for
f,g\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
followed by the inversion formula
f(b)=\frac{1}{2\pi C_{\psi,\alpha}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left((\mathcal{W}_{\psi,\alpha}f)(a,\cdot)\otimes_{\alpha}\psi_{a}\right)(b)\frac{da}{a^{2}},\penalty\ a.e.

With respect to
\psi\in L^{1}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})\cap L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying the admissibility condition i.e.,
0<C_{\psi,\theta}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{|(\mathfrak{F}_{\theta}\psi)(\xi)|^{2}}{|\xi|}d\xi<\infty,
where
\mathfrak{F}_{\theta}
denotes the QFrFT of type II given by equation (
150
), Gupta et al.
[
89
]
introduced the QFrWT of type II of
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})
as

\displaystyle(W^{\theta}_{\psi}f)(a,b)=\left(f\otimes(U(\psi_{a,\theta}))\right)(b),\penalty\ a,b\in\mathbb{R},

(356)

where
\psi_{a,\theta}(t)=\frac{1}{|a|^{\frac{1}{2\theta}}}\psi\left(\frac{t}{(\sgn a)|a|^{\frac{1}{\theta}}}\right),

\otimes
denotes the convolution given by equation (
142
) and
U(f)=\check{\overline{f_{1}}}-jf_{2},
for
f=f_{1}+jf_{2}.
Moreover, if
\psi=\psi_{1}+j\psi_{2},
then the definition (
356
) can be equivalently written as

W^{\theta}_{\psi}f=W^{\theta}_{\psi_{1}}f_{1}+W^{\theta}_{\check{\overline{\psi_{2}}}}\check{\overline{f_{2}}}+j\left(W^{\theta}_{\psi_{1}}f_{2}-W^{\theta}_{\check{\overline{\psi_{2}}}}\check{\overline{f_{1}}}\right).

Thus, as a consequence of which the translation and scaling formula of the transform is given respectively by

(W^{\theta}_{\psi}(\tau_{y}f))(a,b)=\left(W^{\theta}_{\psi}(f_{1}+jf_{2})\right)(b-y,a)-j\left(W^{\theta}_{\check{\overline{\psi_{2}}}}\left(\check{\overline{f_{1}}}+j\check{\overline{f_{1}}}\right)\right)(b+y,a)

and
(W^{\theta}_{\psi}f_{\lambda})(a,b)=(W^{\theta}_{\psi}f)(\lambda^{\theta}a,\lambda b),\penalty\ \lambda>0,\penalty\ f_{\lambda}(t)=\sqrt{\lambda}f(\lambda t).
Apart from this, authors in
[
89
]
obtained the inner product relation, using the convolution theorem and the Parseval’s formula of the FrFT (
150
), which is given by

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(W^{\theta}_{\psi}f)(a,b)\left((W^{\theta}_{\psi}g)(a,b)\right)^{c}\frac{dadb}{|a|^{\frac{1}{\theta}+1}}=C_{\psi,\theta}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(t)(g(t))^{c}dt

followed by the inversion formula
f(t)=\frac{1}{C_{\psi,\theta}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left((\mathcal{W}_{\psi,\alpha}f)(a,\cdot)\otimes_{\alpha}\psi_{a,\theta}\right)(b)\frac{da}{a^{\frac{1}{\theta}+1}}
in
L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}).
With the assumption that the function
\psi
is complex valued, authors also proved that the range
W^{\theta}_{\psi}\left(L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})\right)
is a RKHS with the kernel
K_{\psi,\theta}(a_{0},b_{0};a,b)=\frac{1}{C_{\psi,\theta}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{a,b,\theta}(t)\overline{\psi_{a_{0},b_{0},\theta}(t)}dt,
where
\psi_{a,b,\theta}(t)=\frac{1}{|a|^{\frac{1}{2\theta}}}\psi\left(\frac{t-b}{(\sgn a)|a|^{\frac{1}{\theta}}}\right).
Using the UP (
151
), author obtained the Heisenberg’s type UP for the QWT (
356
) as

\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|b|^{2}|(W^{\theta}_{\psi}f)(b,a)|^{2}\frac{dbda}{|a|^{\frac{1}{\theta}+1}}\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\xi|^{\frac{3}{\theta}-1}|(\mathfrak{F}_{\theta}f)(\xi)|^{2}d\xi\right)\geq\frac{\pi\theta}{4}C_{\psi,\theta}\|f\|^{4}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})}

for
f=f_{1}+jf_{2},\penalty\ \psi=\psi_{1}+j\psi_{2}
are such that
f_{l},\psi_{l},\penalty\ l=1,2
are in the Schwartz class.

26
Quaternion Windowed Linear Canonical transform (QWLCT)

Due to the non-commutativity of the multiplication of quaternions, there are three different types of QWLCT depending on the position of the kernel of the LCT. Gao et al.
[
73
]
introduced the two-sided QWLCT of the function
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with respect to the window function
\phi\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\setminus\{0\}
is defined by

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f\right)(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{u})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}K^{i}_{M_{1}}(x_{1},\omega_{1})f(\boldsymbol{x})\overline{\phi(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{u})}K^{j}_{M_{2}}(x_{2},\omega_{2})d\boldsymbol{x}

(357)

where the kernels
K^{i}_{M_{1}}(x_{1},\omega_{1})
and
K^{j}_{M_{1}}(x_{2},\omega_{2})
are given respectively by (
155
) and (
156
) with

M_{l}=(A_{l},B_{l},C_{l},D_{l})\in\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}
are matrix parameters such that
det(M_{l})=1,
for
l=1,2
and
f\in L^{1}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).
The transform (
357
) is
\mathbb{R}-
linear and is such that
\left|\left(\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f\right)(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{u})\right|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2\pi\sqrt{|B_{1}B_{2}|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.
Apart from the formula of the transform (
357
), for the translation and modulation of a function, given by

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}\tau_{\boldsymbol{r}}f\right)(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{u})=e^{ir_{1}\omega_{1}C_{1}}e^{-i\frac{A_{1}r_{1}^{2}}{2}C_{1}}\left(\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f\right)(\boldsymbol{m},\boldsymbol{n})e^{jr_{2}\omega_{2}C_{2}}e^{-j\frac{A_{2}r_{2}^{2}}{2}C_{2}},

where
\tau_{\boldsymbol{r}}f(\boldsymbol{x})=f(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{r}),\penalty\ \boldsymbol{r}=(r_{1},r_{2}),\penalty\ \boldsymbol{m}=(m_{1},m_{2}),\penalty\ \boldsymbol{n}=(n_{1},n_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{1},m_{l}=\omega_{l}-A_{l}r_{l},n_{l}=u_{l}-r_{l},\penalty\ l=1,2
and

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}\mathbb{M}_{\boldsymbol{s}}f\right)(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{u})=e^{i\omega_{1}s_{1}D_{1}}e^{-i\frac{B_{1}D_{1}s_{1}^{2}}{2}}\left(\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f\right)(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{u})e^{j\omega_{2}s_{2}D_{2}}e^{-j\frac{B_{2}D_{2}s_{2}^{2}}{2}},

where
\mathbb{M}_{\boldsymbol{s}}f(\boldsymbol{x})=e^{ix_{1}s_{1}}f(\boldsymbol{x})e^{jx_{2}s_{2}},

\boldsymbol{s}=(s_{1},s_{2}),

\boldsymbol{v}=(v_{1},v_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

v_{l}=\omega_{l}-s_{l}B_{l},\penalty\ l=1,2
authors in
[
73
]
, obtianed the inversion formula for the QWLCT (
357
) as

f(\boldsymbol{x})=\frac{1}{\|\phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R})}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}K^{-i}_{M_{1}}(x_{1},u_{1})\left(\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}\mathbb{M}_{\boldsymbol{s}}f\right)(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{u})K^{-j}_{M_{2}}(x_{2},\omega_{2})\phi(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{u})d\boldsymbol{\omega}d\boldsymbol{u}.

For the window functions
\phi,\psi
and
f,g\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),
then the transform (
357
), satisfies the orthogonality relation
\langle\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f,\mathcal{G}^{ij,\psi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}g\rangle=Sc[(f,g)(\phi,\psi)].
In particular, if
f=g
and
\phi=\psi
then
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\left(\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f,\mathcal{G}\right)(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{u})\right|^{2}d\boldsymbol{\omega}d\boldsymbol{u}=\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},
i.e.,
\|\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}=\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.
Thus for a given window function
\phi,
the transform
\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}
maps
L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
into
L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}).
If
\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f=0,
then
\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},
so
f=0.
This shows that the transform is one-one. Prasad et al.
[
159
]
characterized the range
\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}(L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
of the transform (
357
). Using the Hausdorff-Young inequality (
162
), authors in
[
159
]
,
[
74
]
proved that for a non-zero function
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),
the transform (
357
) satisfies the Lieb’s inequality

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\mathcal{K}^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{p,q}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\penalty\ p\geq 2

(358)

where
\mathcal{K}^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{p,q}=|B_{1}B_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}A_{l}^{\frac{4}{q}}A_{m^{\prime}}^{\frac{2}{q}}(2\pi)^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{p}},\penalty\ A_{p}=\left(\frac{p^{\frac{1}{p}}}{q^{\frac{1}{q}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\penalty\ l=\frac{p}{q},\penalty\ m=\frac{2}{q}
and
\frac{1}{m}+\frac{1}{m^{\prime}}=1,

using which authors showed that, for a non-zero function
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
\epsilon\geq 0,
if
\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f
is
\epsilon-
concentrated on a measurable subset
\Omega
of
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2},
then
(1-\epsilon^{2})|\Omega|^{\frac{pq}{p-q}}\leq\left(\mathcal{K}^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{p,q}\right)^{2}
for every
p>2.
Using the Pitt’s inequality for the QLCT, authors in
[
159
]
, obtained the inequality for the QWLCT
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\left(\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f\right)(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{u})\right|^{2}d\boldsymbol{\omega}d\boldsymbol{u}\leq\frac{M_{\lambda}}{|B_{1}B_{2}|^{\lambda}}\left\||\boldsymbol{x}|^{\frac{\lambda}{2}}f(\boldsymbol{x})\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

where
M_{\lambda}=\left(\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1-\lambda}{4}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1+\lambda}{4}\right)}\right)^{2},

f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathbb{H}),\penalty\ 0\leq\lambda<2.

Uncertainty Principles:
Apart from this author also gave the local Price’s inequality for the transform as

\left\|\chi_{\Omega}\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f\right\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\frac{|\Omega|^{\frac{1}{p(p+1)}}r^{\frac{2-\epsilon}{p+1}}}{2^{2}(2-\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{2p+2}}}\left\||(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{u})|^{\epsilon}\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f\right\|^{\frac{1}{p+1}}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}+|\Omega|^{\frac{1}{p(p+1)}}\left\||(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{u})|^{\epsilon}\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f\right\|^{\frac{2}{p+1}}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}r^{-\frac{2\epsilon}{p+1}},

where
r>0,

0<\epsilon<2
and
p>1.

Gao et al.
[
74
]
gave the logarithmic UP for the QWLCT (
357
). Using the Jensen’s inequality and the UP, they also obtained its Heisenberg-Weyl’s UP.
With the assumption that
|B_{1}B_{2}|\geq\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}},
they also obtained the Entropy UP

\displaystyle E\left(\left|\mathcal{G}^{ij,\phi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f\right|^{2}\right)\geq\left[\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\left(\ln(2)-\ln(2\pi)-\ln(|B_{1}B_{2}|)\right)-\ln\left(\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\right)\right]\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

(359)

for
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).

27
Quaternion Linear Canonical Wavelet Transform (QLCWT)

A function
\psi\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
is said to be an admissible quaternion linear canonical wavelet if it satisfies the admissibility condition
0<c_{\psi}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\int_{SO(2)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left|\left(\mathcal{L}_{M_{1},M_{2}}\psi^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b}}(\boldsymbol{x})\right)(\boldsymbol{\omega})\right|^{2}\frac{dad\theta}{a}<\infty
a.e.
\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.
Shah et al.
[
178
]
, defined the unitary linear operator
U_{a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\rightarrow L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathcal{G})
as
U_{a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b}}(\psi)=\psi^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b}}(\boldsymbol{x})=\frac{1}{a}e^{-i\frac{A_{1}}{2B_{1}}(x_{1}^{2}-b_{1}^{2})}\psi\left(r_{-\theta}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{b}}{a}\right)\right)e^{-j\frac{A_{2}}{2B_{2}}(x_{2}^{2}-b_{2}^{2})},\penalty\ a\in\mathbb{R}^{+},\boldsymbol{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
r_{-\theta}\in SO(2)
and called the family
\psi^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b}}
as the quaternion daughter linear canonical wavelets. They defined the QLCWT of
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with respect to the quaternion admissible linear canonical wavelet
\psi
as

\displaystyle(\mathcal{W}^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{\psi}f)(a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b})=(f,\psi_{a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b}}^{M_{1},M_{2}})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f(\boldsymbol{x})\frac{1}{a}\overline{e^{-i\frac{A_{1}}{2B_{1}}(x_{1}^{2}-b_{1}^{2})}\psi\left(r_{-\theta}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{b}}{a}\right)\right)e^{-j\frac{A_{2}}{2B_{2}}(x_{2}^{2}-b_{2}^{2})}}d\boldsymbol{x}.

(360)

The transform is
\mathbb{H}-
linear and anti-linear in
f
and
\psi
respectively and have the following translation and scaling formula

(\mathcal{W}^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{\psi}f(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{k}))(a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b})=e^{i\frac{A_{1}k_{1}^{2}}{B_{1}}}\left(\mathcal{W}^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{\psi}\left(e^{i\frac{A_{1}k_{1}x_{1}}{B_{1}}}f(\boldsymbol{x})e^{j\frac{A_{2}k_{2}x_{2}}{B_{2}}}\right)\right)(a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b}-\boldsymbol{k})e^{j\frac{A_{2}k_{2}^{2}}{B_{2}}}

and

(\mathcal{W}^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{\psi}f)(a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b})=\frac{1}{\lambda}\left(\mathcal{W}^{M_{1}^{\prime},M_{2}^{\prime}}_{\psi}f(\lambda\boldsymbol{x})\right)(a\lambda,\theta,\lambda\boldsymbol{b}),\penalty\ \lambda>0

where
M_{l}^{\prime}=(A_{l}^{\prime},B_{l}^{\prime},C_{l}^{\prime},D_{l})
such that
\frac{A_{l}^{\prime}}{B_{l}^{\prime}}=\frac{A_{l}}{B_{l}\lambda^{2}},\penalty\ l=1,2.

Apart from these basic properties authors in
[
178
]
obtained the inner product relation

\displaystyle\int_{SO(2)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{+}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\mathcal{W}^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{\psi}f)(a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b})\overline{(\mathcal{W}^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{\psi}g)(a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b})}d\boldsymbol{b}\right)\frac{dad\theta}{a^{3}}=c_{\psi}(f,g)

(361)

for
f,g\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).
In particular if
f=g,
then
\|\mathcal{W}^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{\psi}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathcal{G})}=c_{\psi}\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},
as a result of which it follows that, expect for the factor
c_{\psi},
the transform
\mathcal{W}^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{\psi}
is an isometry from
L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
to
L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathcal{G}).
The reconstruction formula for the transform (
360
) is given by
f(\boldsymbol{x})=\frac{1}{c_{\psi}}\int_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{W}^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{\psi}f)(a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b})\psi^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b}}(\boldsymbol{x})d\lambda(a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b}),
a.e. Moreover, the range of the transform is a RKHS with the kernel
K_{\psi}(a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b};a^{\prime},\theta^{\prime},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime})=\frac{1}{c_{\psi}}(\psi^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b}},\psi^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{a^{\prime},\theta^{\prime},\boldsymbol{b}^{\prime}}).

Uncertainty Principles:
Using the Heisenberg’s and logarithmic UP for the QFT, the authors obtained the Heisenberg’s UP for the transform (
360
) for
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
as

\left(\int_{\mathcal{G}}\left|b^{2}_{l}(\mathcal{W}^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{\psi}f)(a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b})\right|^{2}d\boldsymbol{b}\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\omega_{l}(\mathcal{F}_{q}f)(\boldsymbol{\omega})\right|d\boldsymbol{\omega}\right)\geq\frac{B_{l}^{2}}{4}\sqrt{c_{\psi}}\|f\|^{4}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\penalty\ l=1,2

and also the logarithmic UP for the QWLCT (
360
)

\int_{\mathcal{G}}\ln|\boldsymbol{b}|\left|(\mathcal{W}^{M_{1},M_{2}}_{\psi}f)(a,\theta,\boldsymbol{b})\right|^{2}d\boldsymbol{b}\frac{dad\theta}{a^{3}}+c_{\psi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\ln|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\left|(\mathcal{F}_{q}f)(\boldsymbol{\omega})\right|^{2}d\boldsymbol{\omega}\geq c_{\psi}\left(D+\ln\sqrt{B_{1}^{2}+B_{2}^{2}}\right)\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

for
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathbb{H})
followed by its local UP.

28
Quaternion Linear Canonical Stockwell Transform (QLCST)

Motivated by the definition of the ST (
224
) given by Stockwell et al. in
[
193
]
. Bhat and Dar
[
213
]
introduced a LCST for a
\mathbb{H}-
valued functions. For
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
\psi\in L^{1}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\cap L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
is a non-zero windowed function, the QLCST is defined as

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{S}^{ij,\psi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f\right)(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{u})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}K^{i}_{M_{1}}(x_{1},\omega_{1})f(\boldsymbol{x})\overline{\psi(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\omega})}K^{j}_{M_{2}}(x_{2},\omega_{2})d\boldsymbol{x}

(362)

where the kernels
K^{i}_{M_{1}}(x_{1},\omega_{1})
and
K^{j}_{M_{1}}(x_{2},\omega_{2})
are given respectively by (
155
) and (
156
) with

M_{l}=(A_{l},B_{l},C_{l},D_{l})\in\mathbb{R}^{4}
are parameters such that
A_{l}D_{l}-C_{l}D_{l}=1,
for
l=1,2
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\omega})=1,\penalty\ \forall\penalty\ \boldsymbol{\omega}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.
The proposed QLCST gives rise to a novel QFrST. The authors obtained their fundamental properties, like linearity, parity, translation, and modulation, followed by the orthogonality relation and the reconstruction formula. They also obtained the Heisenberg and logarithmic UPs associated with the QLCST (
362
), respectively, as

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\omega_{l}^{2}\left|\left(\mathcal{S}^{ij,\psi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f\right)(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{u})\right|^{2}d\boldsymbol{\omega}d\boldsymbol{u}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}x_{l}^{2}|f(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\boldsymbol{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\geq\frac{B_{l}C_{\psi}}{2}\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\penalty\ l=1,2.

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\ln|\boldsymbol{\omega}|\left|\left(\mathcal{S}^{ij,\psi}_{M_{1},M_{2}}f\right)(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{u})\right|^{2}d\boldsymbol{\omega}d\boldsymbol{u}+C_{\psi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\ln|\boldsymbol{x}||f(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\boldsymbol{x}\geq\left(\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2})}-\ln 2\right)C_{\psi}\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

where
C_{\psi}
is some constant depending on
\psi.

29
Quaternion Quadratic Phase Windowed Fourier Transform (QQPWFT)

Let
\wedge_{l}=(A_{l},B_{l},C_{l},D_{l},E_{l}),A_{l},B_{l},C_{l},D_{l},E_{l}\in\mathbb{R}\penalty\ \mbox{and}\penalty\ B_{l}\neq 0\penalty\ \mbox{for}\penalty\ l=1,2
. The short time quaternion quadratic phase Fourier transform (STQQPFT) of a function
f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with respect to a quaternion window function (QWF)
g\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\cap L^{\infty}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
is defined by

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}f\right)(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\xi})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathcal{K}^{i}_{\wedge_{1}}(t_{1},\xi_{1})f(\boldsymbol{t})\overline{g(\boldsymbol{t}-\boldsymbol{x})}\mathcal{K}^{j}_{\wedge_{2}}(t_{2},\xi_{2})d\boldsymbol{t},\penalty\ (\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\xi})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2},

(363)

where
\mathcal{K}^{i}_{\wedge_{1}}(t_{1},\xi_{1})
and
\mathcal{K}^{j}_{\wedge_{2}}(t_{2},\xi_{2})
are given by equations (
183
) and (
184
), respectively. The transform (
363
) is linear in
f
with respect to the scalars in
\{x+iy\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x,y\in\mathbb{R}\}
and anti-linear in
\psi
with respect to the scalars
\{x+jy\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x,y\in\mathbb{R}\}.
It is bounded and satisfies
\left\|\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}f\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
followed by the translation formula

\left(\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}(\tau_{\boldsymbol{k}}f)\right)(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\xi})=\phi^{i}_{\wedge_{1}}(k_{1},\xi_{1})\left(\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}f\right)(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}})\phi^{j}_{\wedge_{2}}(k_{2},\xi_{2}),

where
(\tau_{\boldsymbol{k}}f)(\boldsymbol{t})=f(\boldsymbol{t}-\boldsymbol{k}),

\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{x}}=\left(\xi_{1}+\frac{2A_{1}x_{1}}{B_{1}},\xi_{2}+\frac{2A_{2}x_{2}}{B_{2}}\right),

\phi^{i}_{\wedge_{1}}(k_{1},\xi_{1})=e^{-i\left(A_{1}k_{1}^{2}+D_{1}k_{1}+B_{1}k_{1}\xi_{1}-\frac{4A_{1}^{2}C_{1}k_{1}^{2}}{B_{1}^{2}}-\frac{4A_{1}C_{1}k_{1}\xi_{1}}{B_{1}}-\frac{2A_{1}k_{1}}{B_{1}}\right)}

and

\phi^{j}_{\wedge_{2}}(k_{2},\xi_{2})=e^{-j\left(A_{2}k_{2}^{2}+D_{2}k_{2}+B_{2}k_{2}\xi_{2}-\frac{4A_{2}^{2}C_{2}k_{2}^{2}}{B_{2}^{2}}-\frac{4A_{2}C_{2}k_{2}\xi_{2}}{B_{2}}-\frac{2A_{2}k_{2}}{B_{2}}\right)}.

Along with the scaling formula
\left(\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}\right)(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\xi})=\left(\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1}^{\prime},\wedge_{2}^{\prime}}_{\mathbb{H},g}f\right)\left(\frac{1}{\lambda}\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\xi}\right),
where
(f_{\lambda})(\boldsymbol{t})=\frac{1}{\lambda}f\left(\frac{1}{\lambda}\boldsymbol{t}\right),

\wedge_{l}^{\prime}=\left(\lambda^{2}A_{l},\lambda B_{l},C_{l},\lambda D_{l},E_{l}\right),\penalty\ l=1,2.
Authors in
[
88
]
, obtained the Lieb’s inequality for the transform (
363
) given by

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}f\right\|_{L^{q}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\frac{(2\pi)^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{p}}}{|B_{1}B_{2}|^{\frac{1}{q}}}\left(\frac{2}{q}\right)^{\frac{2}{q}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\mbox{for}\penalty\ f\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).

(364)

Apart from this, authors also proved that for quaternion window functions
g_{1},g_{2}
and
f_{1},f_{2}\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),
then
\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g_{1}}f_{1},

\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g_{2}}f_{2}\in L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
and satisfies the following inner product relation

\left\langle\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g_{1}}f_{1},\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g_{2}}f_{2}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{|B_{1}B_{2}|}\langle f_{1}(\overline{g_{1}},\overline{g_{2}}),f_{2}\rangle,
followed by the inversion formula

f(\boldsymbol{t})=\frac{|B_{1}B_{2}|}{\|g\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\overline{\mathcal{K}^{i}_{\wedge_{1}}(t_{1},\xi_{1})}\left(\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}f\right)(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\xi})\overline{\mathcal{K}^{j}_{\wedge_{2}}(t_{2},\xi_{2})}g(\boldsymbol{t}-\boldsymbol{x})d\boldsymbol{x}d\boldsymbol{\xi}.

Bhat et al.
[
41
]
considered the two-sided quaternion QPFT (
363
) where the factor
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}
in the kernels
\mathcal{K}^{i}_{\wedge_{1}}(t_{1},\xi_{1})
and
\mathcal{K}^{j}_{\wedge_{2}}(t_{2},\xi_{2})
are replaced respectively with
\sqrt{\frac{B_{1}i}{2\pi}}
and
\sqrt{\frac{B_{2}j}{2\pi}}.
Let us denote the transform in this case by
{}^{(1)}\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}.
They also obtained the basic properties along with the inversion formula and Plancherel identity for the transform
{}^{(1)}\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}
as in
[
88
]
. With the aid of the Pitt’s inequality for the two-sided QFT and the relation of the QQPWFT with the two-sided QFT, they also obtained the Pitt’s inequality

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\boldsymbol{\xi}|^{-\alpha}\left|\left({}^{(1)}\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}f\right)(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\xi})\right|^{2}d\boldsymbol{x}d\boldsymbol{\xi}\leq\frac{|\boldsymbol{B}|^{\alpha}}{4\pi^{2}}\Delta_{\alpha}\|g\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\boldsymbol{x}|^{\alpha}|f(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\boldsymbol{x},

\Delta_{\alpha}=\pi^{\alpha}\left[\Gamma\left(\frac{2-\alpha}{4}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{2+\alpha}{4}\right)\right],

0\leq\alpha\leq 2
and
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathbb{H}).

Uncertainty Principles:
Using the logarithmic UP for the QQPFT
{}^{(1)}\mathcal{Q}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H}}
given by equations (
190
), authors in
[
41
]
obtained the logarithmic UP for
{}^{(1)}\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}
as

\frac{\|g\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\ln|\boldsymbol{x}||f(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}d\boldsymbol{x}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\ln|\boldsymbol{\xi}|\left|\left({}^{(1)}\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}f\right)(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\xi})\right|^{2}d\boldsymbol{x}d\boldsymbol{\xi}\geq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}(D-\ln|\boldsymbol{B}|)\|g\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

for
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathbb{H}).
Also with the assumption
\|g\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=1
and
\int\int_{\Omega}\left|\left({}^{(1)}\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}f\right)(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\xi})\right|^{2}d\boldsymbol{x}d\boldsymbol{\xi}\geq 1-\epsilon,
for
\Omega
being a measurable subset of
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\epsilon\geq 0
, they proved that the measure
|\Omega|
of
\Omega
is bounded below by
\frac{2\pi(1-\epsilon)}{\sqrt{|B_{1}B_{2}|}}.
In addition, if
0<|\Omega|<1,
then the transform
{}^{(1)}\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}
satisfies the following local uncertainty inequality
1\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-|\Omega|}}\|^{(1)}\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}f\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\Omega^{c})}.
As a consequence of the Lieb’s inequality (
364
), author in
[
88
]
proved the Lieb’s UP, i.e., if
\mathcal{S}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}_{\mathbb{H},g}f,
is
\epsilon-
concentrated on a measurable subset
\Omega
of
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2},
then for every
q>2,

|\Omega|\geq\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{|B_{1}B_{2}|}(1-\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{q}{q-2}}\left(\frac{q}{2}\right)^{\frac{4}{q-2}}.
With the assumption that
\|g\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=1,
authors also proved that the QQPWFT (
363
) satisfies

\mathcal{E}_{S}(f,g,\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2})\geq\frac{2}{|B_{1}B_{2}|},

where
\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{S}(f,g,\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2})=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathbb{H},g}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}f\right)(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\xi})\right|^{2}\log\left(\left|\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathbb{H},g}^{\wedge_{1},\wedge_{2}}f\right)(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{\xi})\right|^{2}\right)d\boldsymbol{x}d\boldsymbol{\xi}.

30
Conclusion

This review is a comprehensive survey of a large class of Fourier-type integral transforms. Looking at the recent interests of researchers, it becomes extremely important for young researchers to have a collective study in this field. Not only the Fourier transform to FrFT, LCT, or QPFT, but also the popular joint time-frequency analyzing tools such as windowed Fourier transform, wavelet transforms, and the Stockwell transforms are generalized in the fractional domain, linear canonical domain, and the quadratic phase domain. This survey paper contains the mathematical properties of all these transforms and their variants. Despite many applications for some transforms, this survey mainly focuses on their mathematical properties. Section-wise presentation for each transform makes it convenient for readers to grasp the direction and find research gaps in the relevant area. We have tried our level best to include almost all the variants of each transform reported in the literature. The pictorial representation given at the beginning serves as the best tool for understanding the direction of the generalization of the transform. Despite several properties and inequalities, like sharp Hausdorff Youngs inequality, Lieb’s inequality, Pitt’s inequality, etc., of the transformation, this review article also presents several qualitative as well as the quantitative UPs like Heisenberg, local, logarithmic, Réyni (in particular, Shannon), Lieb’s UP, etc. Apart from just giving the result obtained in the literature, we have also tried to include the ideas of proofs in short.

Quaternion is the generalization of complex numbers. Recently, researchers have been actively involved in defining and exploring new integral transforms for quaternion-valued functions. Generalization of the transform for functions of complex-valued to quaternion-valued has fascinated researchers for the last two decades. This paper also discusses a non-trivial generalization of the convolutions in the Fourier domain, fractional Fourier domain, linear canonical domain, and quadratic phase Fourier domain to quaternion-valued functions defined on
\mathbb{R}.
This survey paper also discusses the ideas of defining some convolution-based wavelet, and Stockwell transforms in quaternion Fourier, fractional Fourier, linear canonical, and quadratic phase Fourier domains. For the function of quaternion-valued defined on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we observe that several transforms can be defined depending upon the positions of the kernels. This article can serve better for the signal processing engineers in selecting an appropriate tool for the quaternion value signal analysis.

While going through the literature, we noticed that the Fourier analysis and its offshoots have also been explored for the function of Clifford algebra valued, a generalization of the quaternion algebra. Scholars researching the Fourier-type integral transform and its variants for Clifford algebra-valued functions will find this survey to be of great use.
